=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is an N \times N grid. Takahashi wants to color each cell black or white so that all of the following conditions are satisfied:

- For every row, the following condition holds:

- There exists an integer i\ (0\leq i\leq N) such that the leftmost i cells are colored black, and the rest are colored white.

- For every column, the following condition holds:

- There exists an integer i\ (0\leq i\leq N) such that the topmost i cells are colored black, and the rest are colored white.


Out of these N^2 cells, M of them have already been colored. Among them, the i-th one is at the X_i-th row from the top and the Y_i-th column from the left, and it is colored black if C_i is B and white if C_i is W.
Determine whether he can color the remaining uncolored N^2 - M cells so that all the conditions are satisfied.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N M
X_1 Y_1 C_1
\vdots
X_M Y_M C_M

Output

If it is possible to satisfy the conditions, print Yes; otherwise, print No.

Constraints


- 1\leq N\leq 10^9
- 1\leq M\leq \min(N^2,2\times 10^5)
- 1\leq X_i,Y_i\leq N
- (X_i,Y_i)\neq (X_j,Y_j)\ (i\neq j)
- C_i is B or W.
- All input numbers are integers.

Sample Input 1

4 3
4 1 B
3 2 W
1 3 B

Sample Output 1

Yes

For example, one can color the grid as in the following figure to satisfy the conditions. The cells already colored are surrounded by red borders.

Sample Input 2

2 2
1 2 W
2 2 B

Sample Output 2

No

No matter how the remaining two cells are colored, the conditions cannot be satisfied.

Sample Input 3

1 1
1 1 W

Sample Output 3

Yes

Sample Input 4

2289 10
1700 1083 W
528 967 B
1789 211 W
518 1708 W
1036 779 B
136 657 B
759 1497 B
902 1309 B
1814 712 B
936 763 B

Sample Output 4

No


Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's produce final code:

import sys
def main():
    input=sys.stdin.readline
    N,M=map(int,input().split())
    rows={}
    for _ in range(M):
        x,y,c=input().split()
        x=int(x); y=int(y)
        if x not in rows:
            rows[x]=[0,N]  # L, U
        if c=='B':
            if y>rows[x][0]: rows[x][0]=y
        else:
            u=y-1
            if u<rows[x][1]: rows[x][1]=u
    min_u=N+1
    for x in sorted(rows):
        L,U=rows[x]
        if L>U:
            print("No"); return
        if min_u < L:
            print("No"); return
        if U < min_u:
            min_u = U
    print("Yes")

Let's read problem statement: "There is an N x N grid. Takahashi wants to color each cell black or white so that all conditions: For every row, exists integer i (0<=i<=N) such that leftmost i black, rest white. For every column, exists integer i such that topmost i black, rest white. Out of N^2 cells, M already colored... Determine whether can color remaining." Yes.

Now, let's see if there is any nuance: The row condition and column condition are independent with same variable i but can differ per row/column. We used row thresholds r_x and column thresholds c_y. Good.

Let's perhaps derive another way: The black cells form a Ferrers diagram. Given precolored cells, need extend to Ferrers diagram. A Ferrers diagram in matrix orientation with rows top to bottom and columns left to right is a Young diagram (top-left justified). The condition for extendability of a partial Ferrers board is exactly no white cell northwest of a black cell? Wait in Young diagram, if a cell is black, all cells northwest (above-left) are black. Thus any white northwest of black invalid. Is that sufficient for partial Ferrers board? Yes, if no white northwest of black, we can take the downward closure of black cells? Actually black set must be an order ideal: if B at (x,y), all (x'<=x,y'<=y) black. So the minimal required black set is the downward closure of all given B cells. We need ensure no given W lies in this closure. Then color exactly that closure black and rest white? Does that satisfy row/column prefix? The downward closure of a set of cells in product order is a Young diagram (if we include all northwest of B). But is it necessarily row/column prefix? Downward closure of arbitrary B cells is an order ideal: if (x,y) in closure, all northwest in closure. This is exactly a Young diagram with row/column prefixes? Let's check: An order ideal in [N]x[N] with product order (downward closed) is a Ferrers shape, rows are prefixes and columns are prefixes? If (x,y) black, then all (x'<=x,y'<=y) black. Does this imply row x is left prefix? For fixed row x, if y black, then all y'<=y black, so yes prefix. For fixed column y, if x black, all x'<=x black, yes prefix. So any order ideal works. Thus extendable iff the downward closure of B cells contains no W cells. This is exactly no W weakly northwest of B. This is even simpler. Let's verify: If no W is northwest of any B, then define black set as all cells (x',y') such that there exists a given B at (x,y) with x'<=x and y'<=y (plus maybe all black? Actually also uncolored can be black). This set is downward closed and contains all B, avoids all W. Then row/column conditions hold. This is a constructive proof. This avoids row threshold derivation. It also yields same condition: For each B at (x,y), all W at (x_w<=x, y_w<=y) forbidden. Equivalently, for each W at (x_w,y_w), all B at (x_b>=x_w, y_b>=y_w) forbidden. Processing rows increasing, maintain minimum y among W seen so far; if B has y >= min_y_W, fail. Wait "W seen so far" rows <= B row. If min_y_W <= B_y, then there exists W with row <= B row and col <= B col, i.e. W northwest of B. Fail. This is exactly min_col_W <= y. This is perhaps more intuitive and maybe simpler code: group W and B by row? We can process all constraints sorted by row, but need consider W in same row before B. If we sort by row and within row process W before B, we can maintain min_w_col. Let's examine if this fully handles same row W left of B: If same row W before B in processing, min_w_col updated, then B check fails if min_w_col <= y. If same row W right of B, min_w_col > y, no fail. If same row W and B with W left, fail; row condition. If same row multiple W, min. If same row B before W in input, sorting by (x, type) with W before B ensures. But if we group, easier.

Could there be a W northwest of B with same row and same column? Same cell impossible, but if same row same col no. If W at (x,y), B at (x,y) impossible. So no issue.

Let's test the "downward closure" approach with sample4 maybe? We can maybe compute mentally? Not needed.

Which algorithm is more robust? Both. The L/U algorithm is derived from row thresholds and checks min_U < L, where min_U = min(W_y -1). That is equivalent to min_W_y <= L. Since L is max B y in row. Wait if row has multiple B, L=max y. Need no W with col <= any B y. If min_W_y <= max_B_y, fail. If min_W_y > max_B_y, all W cols > all B cols in that row, row feasible. But what about a W in same row with col between two B? Since B set is prefix, if there are B at y=5 and y=3, max=5; W at y=4: min_W_y=4 <=5 fail. Good. If B at y=3 and W at y=4, min_W_y=4 >3 no fail; row prefix can be 3. Good. So L/U check catches same row.

Now, if we process rows sorted and maintain min_col_W among all W rows seen so far (including current if update before), for each B row with max B y L, check min_col_W <= L. Is that sufficient? Suppose row has B at y=5 and W at y=4 same row; min_col_W after update includes 4, check 4<=5 fail. Suppose row has B at y=3 and W at y=4; min_col_W=4, check 4<=3 false. Good. Suppose previous W at y=6, current B at y=5; min_col_W=6, 6<=5 false, okay (W above right of B). Suppose previous W at y=5, current B at y=5; min_col_W=5<=5 fail, W above same column and B below, impossible. Good. Suppose previous W at y=4, current B at y=5; fail. Good.

So we could implement even simpler:
- Use dict row_data: for each row, store min_w_y (N+1) and max_b_y (0).
- For each constraint:
   if B: max_b_y = max(max_b_y, y)
   if W: min_w_y = min(min_w_y, y)
- Sort rows.
- min_w_col = N+1 (minimum y among W in processed rows)
- For each row in sorted order:
   # update with W in this row before checking B? To catch same row W left of B.
   if min_w_y <= N: min_w_col = min(min_w_col, min_w_y)
   if max_b_y > 0 and min_w_col <= max_b_y: No
   # But also need if min_w_y <= max_b_y same row? Already after update. If W right, min_w_col might be from previous W; if no previous, min_w_col = min_w_y > max_b_y, no fail. If previous W min <= max_b_y, fail due previous. Good.
   # Do we need update after B? If we update before B, same row W considered. If we update after B, same row W left would not be caught unless we separately check row. But update before is okay.
- At end Yes.

But wait, what about a row with W left of B and also previous min_w_col > max_b_y? If we update before, min_w_col becomes W left <= max_b_y, fail. Good.
What about a row with W left of B but no B? no check. Fine.
What about a row with W right of B and previous min_w_col > max_b_y? update before min_w_col = min(previous, W_right). If previous > max_b_y and W_right > max_b_y, min > max_b_y, no fail. Good.
What about a row with W left of B but B max maybe smaller than W? impossible if W left means y_w <= y_b, so min_w_y <= max_b_y. fail.

This min_col_W approach maybe avoids computing U and L>U separately. But need ensure if a row has W and B with W left, fail; if W right, okay. The condition "no W northwest of B" is exactly checked by maintaining minimum W column among rows processed so far (including current) and comparing to each B's column. If we only compare to max B per row, is that enough? Suppose row has B at y=5 and B at y=3; max=5. If min_w_col <=5, fail. If min_w_col=4, fail due B at y=5 (W col4 <=5). If min_w_col=6, no fail. If min_w_col=3, fail due B at y=3 or5. If min_w_col=5, fail due B at y=5. So comparing to max B catches any W col <= some B col? If min_w_col <= max_B_y, there exists B with y >= min_w_col (the max), so W northwest of that B. Yes. If min_w_col > max_B_y, then all W cols > all B cols, so no W left of any B. Thus max B enough. Good.

But wait, what about W in same row with col greater than max B but less than some? max is largest B, so if W col > max, it's to right of all B, okay. If W col <= max, it's left of or equal to the max B, invalid. So yes.

This min_col_W algorithm is perhaps simpler and directly tied to forbidden pattern. Let's compare with L/U algorithm:
L/U algorithm:
min_U = min(W_y -1) among previous rows.
For row with L=max_B_y:
 if min_U < L -> No
 update min_U = min(min_U, U=min_W_y -1)
This checks previous W only, and same row via L>U. If we update min_U after check, same row W not included in min_U for same row; L>U catches same row. Equivalent.
min_col_W algorithm:
min_w_col = min(W_y) among processed rows including current.
For row with max_B_y:
 if min_w_col <= max_B_y -> No
 update before check includes same row. It doesn't need separate L>U. But is there a case where same row W right of B but previous W none, min_w_col = W_right > max_B, no fail; okay. Same row W left, fail. Same row W same as B impossible. So yes.

Which is less error-prone? Let's test min_col_W with a row that has W y=5 and B y=3, N=5. min_w_col=5, max_B=3, 5<=3 false, Yes. Row prefix r=3 works: B at col3 black, W at col5 white. Good.
Row has W y=3 and B y=5, min=3<=5 fail. Row prefix cannot have B at 5 and W at 3. Good.
Row has W y=4 and B y=4? impossible same cell, but if W at 4 and B at 5? min=4<=5 fail. If B at 3 and W at4, okay. So row condition exactly min_W_y > max_B_y. Good.

Now, if we process rows sorted, and update min_w_col before checking B, then min_w_col includes W in current row. But what about W in current row that is to the right of all B (so no fail) but if there is a later B in a lower row with column smaller than this W? For later row, this W is above; if later B col <= W col, fail. That is correct: W above and not right of B? Actually W at row current, col 5; later B at row lower, col 3. W is above and right of B (col5 > col3), so not northwest; should be allowed. Our min_w_col=5; later B max=3; 5<=3 false, no fail. Good. If later B col=5, W above same col, fail (5<=5). If later B col=6, W above left, fail (5<=6). Good.
What if W current row col5, later row has B max=5 and B max? fail. Good.

Thus min_col_W algorithm is correct and maybe easier to explain in code? But final code only. Need decide which to code. Both O(M log M). The min_col_W algorithm requires grouping W min and B max per row. It doesn't require computing U = min_W_y -1, but same. Let's think about edge case N=1, W at (1,1): row min_w=1,max_b=0. Sort row: update min_w_col=1; max_b=0; if max_b>0? 0>0 false. Yes. B at (1,1): min_w=N+1, max_b=1; update min_w_col=N+1 (no W); if max_b>0 and min_w_col<=1? N+1<=1 false. Yes. If N=1, min_w_col=N+1=2. Good.
If row has no W, min_w_y=N+1; updating min_w_col with N+1 no effect. If min_w_col initialized N+1. Good.

Let's compare with L/U algorithm for a case where row has W and B but W right, and previous min_w_col smaller than max_B? Example previous W col2, current row B col5, W col6. min_w_col after update = min(2,6)=2; max_B=5; 2<=5 fail due previous W above-left of current B. Correct. If previous W col2 and current row B col1, W col6: min=2, max_B=1, 2<=1 false. Previous W col2 is above and right of B col1, allowed. Correct.

Now, let's consider if "no W northwest of B" is indeed necessary and sufficient for a Ferrers board. Let's prove to myself:
- Necessary: In any valid coloring, black set is downward closed: if cell (x,y) black, then all cells (x'<=x, y'<=y) black. Why? Row x has black prefix length r_x >= y. For any x'<=x, need show (x',y) black. Column y has black prefix length c_y >= x (because (x,y) black). Thus for x'<=x, (x',y) black. Then row x' has black prefix length r_{x'} >= y. Then for y'<=y, (x',y') black. So yes. Thus a W at (x',y') with x'<=x, y'<=y would contradict B at (x,y). So no W northwest of B.
- Sufficient: If no W northwest of B, define S = {(x,y) | exists B at (a,b) with x<=a, y<=b}. This is the downward closure of all B cells. It contains all B. It contains no W because if W at (x,y) in S, there exists B at (a,b) with x<=a, y<=b, i.e. W northwest of B, forbidden. S is downward closed by construction. A downward closed set in grid has row prefixes and column prefixes: For any row x, if (x,y) in S, then for y'<=y, (x,y') in S (since same x<=a, y'<=b), so row is left prefix. For any column y, if (x,y) in S, then for x'<=x, (x',y) in S, so column is top prefix. Thus valid. This proof is elegant. It avoids row thresholds. The condition can be checked by scanning rows top to bottom: For each B, if there is any W in rows <= current row and columns <= B column, fail. Maintain minimum column among W seen so far. If min_w_col <= B_col, fail. Since if min_w_col <= B_col, there exists W with row <= current (because seen) and col <= B_col. If min_w_col > B_col, no seen W has col <= B_col. Good. Need process rows in increasing row order, and within same row, W must be considered before B. If we group row and update W before checking B, okay. What about W in same row but below? same row no below. Good.

This proof also shows no need to consider W after B in same row? We process W before B to catch W left. If W right, min_w_col > B_col, no fail. If we processed B before W, same row W left would be missed unless we separately check row. So update W before B.

Let's think if min_w_col should include W in current row before checking B if W is to the right of B? It won't fail. If W is to the left, fail. So yes.

Implementation with min_col_W:
```
import sys
def main():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    N, M = map(int, input().split())
    rows = {}
    for _ in range(M):
        x,y,c = input().split()
        x=int(x); y=int(y)
        if x not in rows:
            rows[x] = [N+1, 0]  # min W y, max B y
        if c == 'W':
            if y < rows[x][0]:
                rows[x][0] = y
        else:
            if y > rows[x][1]:
                rows[x][1] = y
    min_w_col = N+1
    for x in sorted(rows):
        min_w, max_b = rows[x]
        if min_w <= N:
            if min_w < min_w_col:
                min_w_col = min_w
        if max_b > 0 and min_w_col <= max_b:
            print("No")
            return
    print("Yes")
```
Need ensure if row has no W, min_w=N+1. If update min_w_col with N+1 no effect. If row has no B, max_b=0, no check. If row has B y=0? impossible, so max_b>0 iff B. Good.

But wait: What if N=1 and min_w_col initialized N+1=2. If no W, min_w_col=2. If B max_b=1, 2<=1 false. Good. If W at y=1, min_w_col=1, max_b=0 no check. If both impossible. Good.

Implementation:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    M = int(next(it))
    rows = {}
    for _ in range(M):
        x = int(next(it)); y = int(next(it)); c = next(it)
        if x not in rows:
            rows[x] = [N+1, 0]  # min W y, max B y
        if c == b'W':
            if y < rows[x][0]:
                rows[x][0] = y
        else:
            if y > rows[x][1]:
                rows[x][1] = y
    min_w_col = N+1
    for x in sorted(rows):
        min_w, max_b = rows[x]
        if min_w < min_w_col:
            min_w_col = min_w
        if max_b and min_w_col <= max_b:
            print("No")
            return
    print("Yes")
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's ensure no syntax errors:
```
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    M = int(next(it))
    rows = {}
    for _ in range(M):
        x = int(next(it))
        y = int(next(it))
        c = next(it)
        if x not in rows:
            rows[x] = [N + 1, 0]
        if c == b'W':
            if y < rows[x][0]:
                rows[x][0] = y
        else:
            if y > rows[x][1]:
                rows[x][1] = y

    min_w_col = N + 1
    for x in sorted(rows):
        min_w, max_b = rows[x]
        if min_w < min_w_col:
            min_w_col = min_w
        if max_b and min_w_col <= max_b:
            print("No")
            return
    print("Yes")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Wait downward closure of B at (x'', y'') includes all cells with row <= x'' and col <= y''. Since x' > x'', row x' is not included. So no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, and B at row x col y; invalid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, but row x' has no B; algorithm fail when B at row x processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, but row x' is processed after row x? No, row order sorted. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a B at row x col y and W at row x' < x col y' < y, but row x has no B? contradiction.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, and B at row x col y, but row x has W same row left of B? If row x also has W at col y'' < y, algorithm would fail due same row W too. If not, fail due previous W. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, and B at row x col y, but there is another W with min_w_col > y; min_w_col is min, so if y' < y, min_w_col <= y' < y, fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' < y, but y' is not the minimum W col; min_w_col even smaller, fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and no W with col <= y; min_w_col > y, no fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, but there is a B at row x col y and also a B at row x col y'' > y'. Then min_w_col = y' <= y'' fail due B at y''. Is that invalid? W at row above col y', B same row col y'' > y'. W is above-left of B (row x'<x, col y'<y''), invalid. Yes. Our algorithm checks max_b = y'' and min_w_col <= y'' fail. Good.
If B at row x col y'' < y', W above-right, allowed. max_b maybe y (if y<y'), min_w_col y' <= y false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' between y and y'? Then W above-left of that B if y'' >= y'? If y'' < y', W above-right, allowed. max_b might be y'' < y', no fail. If y'' > y', fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' (same col), fail. Algorithm min_w_col=y', B max=y'', y'<=y'' fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' > y', fail. If y'' < y', allowed. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' < y, allowed. Algorithm min_w_col=y', B max=y''<y<y', y'<=y'' false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y, allowed? W col y' > y, so W above right of B, allowed. Algorithm y'<=y false. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' - 1, allowed. y'<=y'-1 false. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' + 1, fail. y'<=y'+1 true. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' also has W left? If row x'' has W left, algorithm would fail due same row W before B maybe. But if B at y' and W at y''<y', min_w_col becomes min(previous, y'') <= y' fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' has W right, min_w_col remains y' (from previous) and B max y', fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' has no W, fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' has another B at col y''' < y', max_b=y', fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' has another B at col y''' > y', max_b=y''', fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' has no B? contradiction.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' is not processed? It will be processed. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and no later B with col >= y', then no fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but there is also a W at row x''' between x' and x'' with col smaller, min_w_col smaller, fail anyway. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and later B at row x'' > x col y'' = y' but row x'' is before row x? no.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x' has no B; algorithm fail? Wait W col y' > y, B col y. min_w_col = y' (assuming no smaller). At row x, max_b=y. Check y' <= y? false. No fail. Good. This is the allowed case W above right of B. Example row1 W col5, row2 B col3. Feasible. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' > y'. Then max_b=y''. min_w_col=y' <= y'' fail. Is it invalid? W above col y', B same row col y'' > y': W above-left of that B, yes invalid. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' = y' (same col as W). W above same col, invalid. min_w_col=y' <= y'' fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' < y' but > y. W above-right of that B, allowed. max_b=y''<y', no fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' < y. max_b=y (if y>y'') maybe y<y', no fail. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has another B at col y'' > y' and another W at col y''' < y''. min_w_col maybe min(y', y''') <= y'' fail. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has no other B. Feasible. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x' has a B at col y'' > y'. Then row x' has B and W, W left of B, invalid. If we had updated W after B, would miss; but we update before. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a W at row x' < x col y' > y, and B at row x col y, but row x has W same row right of B at col
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    it = iter(data)
    N = int(next(it))
    M = int(next(it))

    rows = {}

    for _ in range(M):
        x = int(next(it))
        y = int(next(it))
        c = next(it)

        if x not in rows:
            rows[x] = [N + 1, 0]  # min W column, max B column

        if c == b'W':
            if y < rows[x][0]:
                rows[x][0] = y
        else:
            if y > rows[x][1]:
                rows[x][1] = y

    min_w_col = N + 1

    for x in sorted(rows):
        min_w, max_b = rows[x]

        if min_w < min_w_col:
            min_w_col = min_w

        if max_b and min_w_col <= max_b:
            print("No")
            return

    print("Yes")

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
